> # CHIEF OF STAFF OS
> # MASTER COMPLETENESS, RELATIONSHIP, JOURNEY, AUDIT-LEARNING,
> # AND PHASE 2.5 REQUALIFICATION
> 
> ## ROLE
> 
> Act as an independent audit and systems-reasoning team for Venkat Gullapalli's Chief of Staff OS.
> 
> You are auditing two things:
> 
> 1. the Chief of Staff OS
> 2. the audit process used to understand the Chief of Staff OS
> 
> Your job is to determine whether the evidence, outcomes, applications, relationships, dependencies, journeys, feedback loops, and prior learning are complete enough to support Phase 3.
> 
> Do not optimize for agreement with:
> 
> - prior audit conclusions
> - prior target-state conclusions
> - Venkat's current hypotheses
> - advisor conclusions
> - project conversations
> - historical recommendations
> - existing implementation
> - repository folder structure
> 
> Repository canon and qualified evidence outrank all of those.
> 
> Do not treat this prompt as evidence.
> 
> Do not treat agreement with prior reasoning as confirmation.
> 
> The final result must stop at a new Phase 2.5 human checkpoint.
> 
> DO NOT execute Phase 3, Phase 4, Phase 5, Phase 6, or implementation.
>

>

>

> 
> # CORE AUDIT PRINCIPLES
> 
> ## 1. Evidence before architecture
> 
> For outcome discovery use:
> 
> purpose + evidence + capabilities
> → possible valuable outcomes
> → applications
> → qualification
> 
> Do not derive permanent structure from:
> 
> - folder names
> - file volume
> - number of prompts
> - number of agents
> - current activity
> - existing implementation
> - historical architecture
> - frequency of mentions
> 
> ---
> 
> ## 2. Independent derivation protects against anchoring
> 
> Derive the independent candidate space before introducing:
> 
> - advisor charter
> - project conversations
> - Provided hypotheses
> - historical target-state conclusions
> - prior Phase 3–5 recommendations
> 
> Freeze independent outputs before later challenge passes.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## 3. Completeness reconciliation protects against omission
> 
> Independent derivation alone is insufficient.
> 
> After independent sets are frozen, challenge them against:
> 
> - full repository coverage
> - audit history
> - project conversation history
> - relevant research
> - prior reasoning
> - Provided hypotheses
> - journeys
> - relationships
> - graph structure
> 
> Both anti-anchoring and completeness reconciliation are mandatory.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## 4. A row is not a system
> 
> A phase is not qualified merely because every individual row is defensible.
> 
> Material objects must also be:
> 
> - vertically traceable
> - horizontally connected where evidence supports connection
> - tested against end-to-end journeys
> - tested for feedback loops
> - tested for upstream and downstream consequences
> - tested for cross-stream effects
> - explicitly dispositioned when no relationship is found
> 
> ---
> 
> ## 5. Relationship depth is a search obligation, not an edge quota
> 
> Do not manufacture relationships.
> 
> For every material object, search at least two hops in every materially relevant direction.
> 
> Extend to a third hop where consequence warrants it.
> 
> "None supported" and "Unknown" are valid findings.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## 6. Complexity must earn its place
> 
> Do not build a graph database.
> 
> Do not create a new permanent store.
> 
> Do not create branches, agents, Skills, services, dashboards, CRMs, funnels, landing pages, or other architecture merely because they appear useful.
> 
> Use the simplest audit mechanism that preserves decision quality.
> 
> Graph analysis is an audit method here, not a predetermined target mechanism.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## 7. Protect Venkat's attention
> 
> The audit itself has a quality obligation.
> 
> Treat these as audit-quality failures where avoidable:
> 
> - repeated human checkpoints
> - Venkat acting as the omission detector
> - preventable reruns
> - unnecessarily expensive model usage
> - repeated full-corpus reasoning
> - asking Venkat questions evidence can answer
> - producing work that must be regenerated because upstream coverage was weak
> 
> ---
> 
> # EVIDENCE CLASSIFICATION
> 
> Every material statement must be classified as one of:
> 
> - Observed
> - Provided
> - Supported inference
> - Unknown
> - Hypothesis
> 
> Do not silently convert inference into fact.
> 
> Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence.
> 
> ---
> 
> # AUTHORITY / SOURCE HIERARCHY
> 
> Determine the repository's actual authority model from canon.
> 
> At minimum distinguish:
> 
> 1. canonical governing evidence
> 2. current qualified audit evidence
> 3. current operational evidence
> 4. repository research/reference evidence
> 5. historical/superseded evidence
> 6. project-conversation/process evidence
> 7. advisor reasoning
> 8. Provided hypotheses
> 9. external research
> 10. unsupported inference
> 
> Do not promote a lower-authority source merely because it is newer or more detailed.
> 
> ---
>

>

>

>

> - relevant advisor charter

>

>

>

> 
> ---
> 
> # PHASE 2 RULE
> 
> Do NOT automatically rerun Phase 2.
> 
> Phase 2 was previously qualified.
> 
> Only supplement or correct Phase 2 if this run finds MATERIAL CURRENT-STATE EVIDENCE that:
> 
> 1. existed or should reasonably have been considered by Phase 2,
> 2. was not represented,
> 3. materially changes Phase 2.5 reasoning.
> 
> If this occurs:
> 
> - identify the exact evidence
> - explain why it belongs in Phase 2
> - explain why the prior Phase 2 missed it
> - identify downstream blast radius
> - create a versioned correction/addendum
> - preserve the prior qualified Phase 2 output
> - use the corrected evidence for Phase 2.5
> 
> Do not alter Phase 2 because a new target-state idea sounds valuable.
> 
> ---
> 
> # COST AND MODEL DISCIPLINE
> 
> Do not use the strongest or most expensive reasoning model for deterministic work such as:
> 
> - repository enumeration
> - file metadata
> - hashes
> - path validation
> - CSV manipulation
> - joins
> - duplicate detection
> - link extraction
> - basic reference extraction
> - graph traversal
> - centrality calculation
> - component detection
> - validation
> - counting
> - simple consistency checks
> - git operations
> 
> Use deterministic software for deterministic operations.
> 
> Reserve expensive reasoning for:
> 
> - synthesis
> - disconfirmation
> - contradiction analysis
> - qualification
> - causal reasoning
> - relationship interpretation
> - journey reconstruction
> - outcome discovery
> - AI-native reasoning
> - innovation reasoning
> - root-cause analysis
> - difficult research transfer
> - generation-gap analysis
> 
> Do not send the complete repository or conversation corpus to multiple expensive agents unnecessarily.
> 
> Create bounded evidence packets.
> 
> Parallelize only non-overlapping work.
> 
> ---
> 
> # RELATIONSHIP MODEL
> 
> Use the minimum sufficient relationship vocabulary.
> 
> Prefer:
> 
> - supports
> - depends_on
> - informs
> - constrains
> - produces
> - consumes
> - routes_to
> - feeds_back_to
> - validates
> - challenges
> - owns
> - governs
> - enables
> - conflicts_with
> - supersedes
> 
> Add another relationship type only if it materially changes interpretation.
> 
> For dependency strength use where relevant:
> 
> - hard
> - soft
> - sequencing
> - feedback
> 
> ---
> 
> # RELATIONSHIP DEPTH RULE
> 
> For every material:
> 
> - finding
> - capability
> - workflow
> - outcome
> - application
> - hypothesis
> - journey
> - evidence flow
> - state transition
> 
> perform the following.
> 
> ## Upstream
> 
> Hop 1:
> 
> What directly creates, supports, constrains, enables, or causes this?
> 
> Hop 2:
> 
> What creates, supports, constrains, enables, or causes those upstream objects?
> 
> ## Downstream
> 
> Hop 1:
> 
> What directly consumes, depends upon, changes, or becomes possible because of this?
> 
> Hop 2:
> 
> What does that downstream object then affect?
> 
> ## Cross-stream
> 
> Also ask:
> 
> - what other function informs this?
> - what functions does this inform?
> - what produces its inputs?
> - what consumes its outputs?
> - what evidence crosses boundaries?
> - what state crosses boundaries?
> - where does canonical ownership change?
> - where does authority change?
> - what feedback returns?
> - which journeys contain this relationship?
> 
> ## Mandatory third-hop expansion
> 
> Search at least three hops when the path touches:
> 
> - consequential human authority
> - shared or canonical state
> - another operating stream
> - public users
> - prospects
> - customers
> - collaborators
> - revenue
> - commercial commitment
> - privacy/confidentiality
> - public publication
> - automated writing
> - memory promotion
> - difficult-to-reverse change
> - large blast radius
> 
> Do not force a relationship to exist.
> 
> ---
> 
> # GRAPH-THEORY / GRAPH-ENGINEERING METHOD
> 
> Use typed graphs as analysis instruments.
> 
> Possible node types:
> 
> - sources
> - files
> - decisions
> - evidence
> - findings
> - capabilities
> - workflows
> - outcomes
> - applications
> - hypotheses
> - actors
> - state
> - memories
> - journeys
> - public surfaces
> - offers
> - research references
> 
> Graph diagnostics should include where useful:
> 
> - connected components
> - disconnected clusters
> - in-degree/out-degree
> - bridge edges
> - articulation points
> - betweenness centrality
> - unexplained sources
> - unexplained sinks
> - cycles
> - strongly connected components
> - missing return edges
> - duplicate authority
> - conflicting ownership
> - fragile single-edge connections
> - multi-hop dependency paths
> - cross-stream paths
> 
> Do not use centrality or any graph metric as proof of architectural importance.
> 
> Use graph properties to generate questions and detect omissions.
> 
> ---
> 
> # JOURNEY / SERVICE-BLUEPRINT METHOD
> 
> Do NOT enumerate every conceivable UI flow.
> 
> Identify material end-to-end lifecycles.
> 
> A material journey should be analyzed as a cycle where evidence supports it:
> 
> trigger
> → preparation
> → interaction/work
> → value creation
> → observable response/outcome
> → evidence
> → qualification
> → learning
> → changed future behavior
> 
> For every material journey step consider:
> 
> - actor
> - actor objective
> - trigger
> - visible experience
> - Business question
> - Product/value
> - Experience implication
> - backstage AI work
> - deterministic work
> - evidence consumed
> - evidence produced
> - state read
> - state written
> - canonical owner
> - human authority
> - upstream relationships
> - downstream relationships
> - cross-stream relationships
> - failure
> - recovery
> - feedback destination
> - current-state evidence
> - Unknowns
> 
> Do not derive target architecture during the independent journey-discovery pass.
> 
> ---
> 
> # JOURNEY ↔ RELATIONSHIP CROSS-CHECK
> 
> The relationship analysis and journey analysis must independently test one another.
> 
> ## Relationship → Journey
> 
> Every material relationship must either:
> 
> - appear in a material journey
> 
> OR
> 
> - be classified as structural/cross-cutting with an explicit reason why no direct journey manifestation exists.
> 
> ## Journey → Relationship
> 
> Every material journey transition must identify:
> 
> - what moves
> - origin
> - destination
> - owner
> - state/evidence change
> - authority
> - supporting relationship
> 
> ## Outcome → Journey
> 
> Every material outcome must identify:
> 
> - where value appears in one or more journeys
> 
> OR
> 
> - why the outcome is cross-system enabling.
> 
> ## Journey → Outcome
> 
> Every material journey must connect to a qualified valuable outcome.
> 
> A journey with no valuable outcome must be challenged.
> 
> ---
> 
> # PHASE EXECUTION
> 
> Run these passes in order.
> 
> Use fresh contexts where specified.
> 
> Freeze and hash independent results before introducing later-stage material.
> 
> ---
> 
> # PASS 0 — BASELINE AND SAFETY
> 
> 1. Confirm repository root.
> 2. Record git branch.
> 3. Record git status.
> 4. Record current modified/untracked files.
> 5. Identify repository conventions.
> 6. Identify existing audit output conventions.
> 7. Create only the minimum output/work directory needed.
> 8. Do not modify canon.
> 9. Do not modify operational state.
> 10. Do not overwrite prior audit artifacts.
> 11. Do not implement recommendations.
> 
> Save the baseline.
> 
> ---
>

> 
> This pass is mandatory.
> 
> Do not decide relevance before establishing coverage.
> 
> Inventory the ENTIRE repository first.
>

>

> - workflow handoffs

> 
> Identify provisional clusters based on actual contents and relationships.
> 
> Expected examples include:
>

> - evidence/reasoning/learning
> - memory

> 
> ## E. Tiered semantic review
> 
> After census, assign material to:
> 
> ### Tier 1 — Full semantic review
> 
> Fully read artifacts likely to materially affect:
> 
> - purpose
> - outcomes
> - current-state evidence
> - authority
> - canonical ownership
> - major workflows
> - journeys
> - cross-stream relationships
> - commercial/public value
> - evidence/reasoning/learning
> - governance
> - audit methodology
> 
> ### Tier 2 — Targeted semantic review
> 
> Read relevant sections/chunks where census or relationship signals indicate:
> 
> - dependency
> - contradiction
> - missing capability
> - unexpected relationship
> - possible omitted outcome
> - cross-stream effect
> - useful historical reasoning
> 
> ### Tier 3 — Census/disposition only
> 
> Low expected decision relevance may remain census-only if the reason is documented.
> 
> The goal is not equal reading depth.
> 
> The goal is zero silent exclusion of potentially material repository evidence.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## F. Repository-wide omission search
> 
> Ask:
> 
> - What exists in the repository but not in the current audit?
> - What recurring work lacks outcome/application representation?
> - What research influenced prior reasoning but vanished downstream?
> - What workflow lacks a journey?
> - What decision lacks visible downstream treatment?
> - What public/commercial asset lacks a place in the current value model?
> - Which artifacts have many dependencies but little audit representation?
> - Which historical work was correctly superseded?
> - Which historical work lost useful reasoning during supersession?
> - What implemented capability contradicts the audit's claim that the capability does not exist?
> - What claimed capability lacks implementation or workflow evidence?
> - What cross-directory relationships would be missed if folders were reviewed independently?
> - What evidence contradicts Phase 2?
> - What evidence contradicts the prior Phase 2.5?
> 
> Record candidates.
> 
> Do not qualify them yet.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## G. Repository graph diagnostics
> 
> Use the artifact graph to flag:
> 
> - bridge artifacts
> - highly connected artifacts
> - isolated but potentially consequential artifacts
> - nodes with many incoming references
> - nodes with many outgoing dependencies
> - circular chains
> - duplicate authority
> - conflicting versions
> - artifacts whose removal disconnects major clusters
> - cross-directory coupling
> - apparently separate areas sharing state/evidence
> 
> These are discovery signals.
> 
> Do not derive target architecture from them.
> 
> ---
> 
> Freeze and hash:
> 
> - repository census
> - structural artifact graph
> - provisional cluster inventory
> 
> Do not expose downstream target conclusions to independent discovery workers.
> 
> ---
>

> 
> Allowed inputs:
> 
> - canonical governing evidence
> - qualified Phase 1
> - qualified Phase 2

> - repository research only where repository evidence establishes relevance
> 
> EXCLUDE:

>

> 
> # PASS B — INDEPENDENT PHASE 2.5 OUTCOME / APPLICATION DISCOVERY
> 
> Use only:
> 
> - qualified Phase 1
> - qualified Phase 2
> - any valid Phase 2 correction from this run
> - evidence available under the independent rules
> 
> Do not read:
> 
> - old Phase 2.5 conclusions
> - Phase 3+
> - advisor
> - project conversations
> - Provided hypotheses
> 
> Use:
> 
> purpose + evidence + capabilities
> → possible valuable outcomes
> → possible applications
> → qualification candidates
> 
> Search for:
> 
> - valuable current outcomes to preserve
> - negative outcomes that should disappear
> - AI-native redesign
> - new applications of existing capabilities
> - capability recombinations
> - newly viable outcomes
> - outcomes requiring investigation
> - None found
> 
> Classify:
> 
> - Automation
> - AI-native Redesign
> - AI-native Innovation
> - AI-native Innovation Hypothesis
> - None found
> - Unclassified / insufficient evidence
> 
> AI-native Innovation requires:
> 
> 1. materially new application
> 2. materially new valuable outcome
> 3. material AI enablement
> 4. evidence or credible hypothesis supporting value
> 
> Do not classify faster, cheaper, easier, or higher-quality production of the same basic outcome as innovation by itself.
> 
> Freeze and hash the independent candidate set.
> 
> ---
> 
> # PASS C — INDEPENDENT RELATIONSHIP ANALYSIS
> 
> Using only evidence allowed to Passes A and B:
> 
> Build the semantic Cross-System Relationship Register.
> 
> For every material candidate, capability, workflow, and relevant state object:
> 
> - perform upstream two-hop search
> - perform downstream two-hop search
> - perform cross-stream search
> - extend to third hop where required
> 
> Record:
> 
> - source node
> - relationship
> - destination node
> - evidence
> - evidence class
> - confidence
> - dependency strength where relevant
> - hop depth
> - cross-stream status
> - authority boundary
> - Unknown where evidence is insufficient
> 
> Freeze and hash.
> 
> ---
> 
> # PASS D — INDEPENDENT JOURNEY DISCOVERY
> 
> Still exclude:
> 
> - advisor conclusions
> - project conversation corpus
> - Provided journey hypotheses
> - historical Phase 3+ target conclusions
> 
> Independently derive the minimum sufficient set of material end-to-end journeys needed to explain:
> 
> - how work moves
> - how value is created
> - how evidence moves
> - how decisions happen
> - how learning returns
> - how internal users interact
> - how external users interact
> - how relationships progress
> - how public value operates
> - how commercial value operates where supported
> 
> Do not start from conventional funnel models.
> 
> Do not assume all journeys are linear.
> 
> Look for cycles and return paths.
> 
> Run preliminary Journey ↔ Relationship reconciliation.
> 
> Freeze and hash.
> 
> ---
> 
> # PASS E — INDEPENDENT DISCONFIRMATION
> 
> Use a fresh context.
> 
> Challenge Passes A–D.
> 
> Ask:
> 
> - Which candidate outcomes lack sufficient evidence?
> - Which outcomes duplicate one another?
> - Which relationships are plausible but unsupported?
> - Which journeys were inferred from convention?
> - Which candidate lacks a beneficiary?
> - Which supposedly separate things are one lifecycle?
> - Which supposedly connected things have no evidence of connection?
> - Which recurrence claims are actually repeated copies of the same event?
> - Which outcomes are mechanisms disguised as outcomes?
> - Which applications are architecture disguised as capability?
> - Which "AI-native" claims are merely AI presence?
> - Which negative cases weaken the conclusion?
> - What would reverse each material finding?
> - What material area remains disconnected?
> 
> Freeze and hash the challenged set.
> 
> ---
> 
> # PASS F — AUDIT-THE-AUDIT / AUDIT INCIDENT REVIEW
> 
> Now inspect the history of Phases 1 through 5.
> 
> Review:
> 
> - checkpoints
> - corrections
> - superseded audit versions
> - reruns
> - weak gates
> - changed prompts
> - places Venkat rejected or corrected conclusions
> - places work was not deep enough
> - places evidence aperture was too narrow
> - places a conclusion was prematurely downgraded
> - places downstream phases had to be redone
> - places excessive human confirmation was requested
> - places large amounts of model usage were wasted
> - places one local correction should have triggered a general process correction
> 
> For every material incident record:
> 
> - phase
> - original result
> - later correction
> - who detected it
> - evidence exposing it
> - root failure mechanism
> - downstream blast radius
> - repeated work
> - human burden
> - model/computational burden where known
> - control that would have prevented it
> - whether recurrence is required before that control is justified
> 
> Distinguish:
> 
> A. demonstrated high-consequence control failure
> 
> from:
> 
> B. inferred preference/pattern requiring repeated evidence before durable promotion
> 
> Do not require multiple failures before proposing a proportionate safeguard for A.
> 
> Freeze the Audit Incident Register.
> 
> ---
> 
> # PASS G — PROJECT CONVERSATION MINI-AUDIT
> 
> Now introduce:
> 
> /Users/venkatgullapalli/Documents/my-ai-lab-v2/chief-of-staff/reference/conversations/chatgpt-chief-of-staff-os-advisor
> 
> First inventory the corpus deterministically.
> 
> Do not send every conversation wholesale into the strongest model.
> 
> Extract bounded interaction records.
> 
> For material interactions determine where supported:
> 
> - date
> - topic
> - what Venkat asked
> - underlying decision/problem
> - answer/framing offered
> - assumptions in the answer
> - what Venkat accepted
> - what Venkat rejected
> - what Venkat corrected
> - what Venkat refined
> - new distinction exposed
> - new relationship exposed
> - new dependency exposed
> - downstream work changed
> - unresolved issue
> - item that disappeared later
> - apparent advisor learning
> - apparent Venkat learning/refinement
> - audit-process implication
> 
> Use caution with learning claims.
> 
> If Venkat explicitly states a learning/change:
> Observed.
> 
> If a sequence strongly supports refinement:
> Supported inference.
> 
> Otherwise:
> Unknown.
> 
> Do not state "we never discussed X" merely because no keyword appears.
> 
> Absence from conversation is at most a coverage hypothesis until compared against an evidence-backed expected journey or capability.
> 
> ---
> 
> # PASS G2 — CONVERSATION GRAPH ANALYSIS
> 
> Create a temporal typed interaction/concept graph.
> 
> Look for:
> 
> - concepts repeatedly reintroduced by Venkat
> - concepts repeatedly corrected
> - bridge concepts
> - high-betweenness concepts
> - articulation points in reasoning
> - corrections with large downstream blast radius
> - early concepts that disappeared in later audit phases
> - unresolved questions that vanished
> - concept clusters connected in conversation but disconnected in audit outputs
> - repeated "locally correct, globally incomplete" patterns
> - places Venkat became the integration mechanism
> - places the advisor learned but the audit methodology did not
> - concepts with low mention count but high decision consequence
> 
> Compare:
> 
> CONVERSATION DECISION IMPORTANCE
> 
> against:
> 
> AUDIT REPRESENTATION
> 
> Do not use raw frequency as the main measure.
> 
> Freeze the Conversation Learning Register.
> 
> ---
> 
> # PASS H — ADVISOR / PRIOR-REASONING CHALLENGE
> 
> Only now read:
> 
> - current Chief of Staff OS Advisor charter
> - current concise project instructions
> - other relevant prior reasoning documents
> 
> Treat them as challenge material, not evidence.
> 
> For every material advisor principle classify:
> 
> - independently represented
> - strengthens an existing candidate
> - challenges an existing candidate
> - exposes a possible generation gap
> - governance principle only
> - mechanism option only
> - unsupported
> - contradicted
> - Unknown
> 
> Agreement is not evidence.
> 
> Freeze the result.
> 
> ---
> 
> # PASS I — PROVIDED HYPOTHESIS CHALLENGE
> 
> Only now introduce the following Provided hypotheses.
> 
> They receive no incumbency advantage.
> 
> For every hypothesis determine whether it is:
> 
> - independently supported
> - partially supported
> - weakened
> - rejected
> - merged into another concept
> - a mechanism rather than outcome
> - a possible target-state question
> - still Unknown
> 
> ## I-1 Shared evidence / reasoning / learning
> 
> There may be a cross-system capability allowing qualified evidence, reasoning, corrections, outcomes, and learning to support:
> 
> - decisions
> - Research
> - Positioning
> - Offer
> - Publishing
> - Public Application Discovery
> - Revenue
> - Delivery
> - Relationships
> - procedural memory
> - anticipation
> - governance
> 
> This does NOT imply a single centralized store.
> 
> Test:
> 
> - distributed canonical ownership
> - references
> - provenance
> - transactive routing
> - promotion rules
> - feedback
> 
> ---
> 
> ## I-2 Positioning
> 
> Positioning may be both:
> 
> - downstream consumer of evidence and market learning
> - upstream context for Research, Offer, Publishing, outreach, proof, and public surfaces
> 
> Test the relationship.
> 
> Do not let Positioning contaminate independent behavioral discovery.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## I-3 Offer
> 
> Offer may bridge:
> 
> evidence + Positioning + market evidence
> → defined problem/buyer/value
> → exposure
> → market response
> → commercial decision
> → Delivery
> → proof
> → learning
> 
> Do not assume a separate Offer department.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## I-4 Public reasoning
> 
> Potential path:
> 
> real work
> → private reasoning/evidence
> → Editorial Reasoning
> → Publishing / Build in Public
> → behavioral evidence
> → qualified learning
> → future reasoning
> 
> Test the path.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## I-5 Public Application Discovery
> 
> Potential path:
> 
> real work/evidence
> → useful application hypothesis
> → bounded test
> → route to appropriate owner
> → behavioral evidence
> → qualified learning
> 
> Do not assume PAD becomes a standing department.
> 
> "None found" remains valid.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## I-6 Audience → relationship → opportunity
> 
> Test whether the OS needs a coherent lifecycle resembling:
> 
> first exposure
> → repeated useful engagement
> → familiarity
> → trust
> → interest/intent
> → owned destination
> → useful proof/experience
> → anonymous or known relationship
> → contact
> → qualification
> → conversation
> → commercial fit
> → Offer
> → commitment
> 
> Do not assume:
> 
> - a funnel
> - a CRM
> - a newsletter sequence
> - landing pages
> - lead scoring
> - automated nurture
> 
> Determine the needed outcome/capability before mechanism.
> 
> Explicitly test:
> 
> - LinkedIn
> - Substack
> - portfolio/owned site
> - GitHub
> - public diagnostics/tools
> - contact surfaces
> - lead/contact capture
> - source attribution
> - consent/privacy
> - canonical ownership of relationship state
> - routing
> - repeated engagement over time
> 
> The question is not merely:
> 
> "Where is the contact form?"
> 
> The question is:
> 
> > What does a person experience from repeated exposure to the point where reaching out becomes the obvious useful next action, and what system capabilities support that transition?
> 
> ---
> 
> ## I-7 Prospect → client → continued value
> 
> Test a lifecycle such as:
> 
> conversation
> → discovery
> → fit
> → Offer
> → scope
> → price
> → commitment
> → onboarding
> → Delivery
> → verified outcome
> → proof
> → follow-through
> → repeat
> → expansion
> → advisory continuation
> → referral
> → re-engagement
> → learning
> 
> Do not assume every opportunity follows every step.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## I-8 Public surfaces
> 
> Test relationships among:
> 
> - LinkedIn
> - Substack
> - portfolio/owned website
> - GitHub
> - public tools
> - diagnostics
> - essays
> - contact/conversion surfaces
> 
> Determine:
> 
> - what role each currently plays
> - what role current evidence suggests it should play
> - what remains Unknown
> 
> Do not design the target website here.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## I-9 Business / Product / Experience
> 
> Test the existing model:
> 
> Business:
> Which people, problems, situations, relationships, and opportunities matter?
> 
> Product:
> What valuable capability, service, method, instrument, analysis, tool, or outcome delivers value?
> 
> Experience:
> How does the person recognize, receive, use, and continue the value?
> 
> Do NOT assume these are departments.
> 
> Do NOT automatically create quality bars.
> 
> Test whether future target-state work should consider:
> 
> - Business quality criteria
> - Product quality criteria
> - shared Experience principles
> - Business Experience
> - Product/Service Experience
> - Internal OS Experience
> - one Experience architecture spanning multiple journeys
> 
> These are Phase 3 questions if supported, not predetermined Phase 2.5 conclusions.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## I-10 Westin Cookie experience hypothesis
> 
> Provided hypothesis:
> 
> At meaningful moments, preparation, memory, follow-through, or useful anticipation should show the person that their context and the specific moment were considered rather than merely processing the transaction.
> 
> Possible manifestations:
> 
> - "He prepared for me."
> - "He was paying attention."
> - "He remembered relevant context."
> 
> Test whether this is best understood as:
> 
> - valuable outcome
> - experience principle
> - requirement candidate
> - later recommendation
> - unsupported aspiration
> 
> Do not manufacture personalized gestures.
> 
> Do not create a gimmicky "cookie" mechanism.
> 
> Do not infer private preferences without evidence.
> 
> Test internal and external relevance.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## I-11 Material journey hypotheses
> 
> Challenge the independently derived journey set against possible families such as:
> 
> - internal signal → decision → action → closure → learning
> - research → evidence → synthesis → application → learning
> - private reasoning → retrieval/reuse → future reasoning
> - Positioning lifecycle
> - Offer lifecycle
> - public reasoning / Publishing lifecycle
> - Build in Public lifecycle
> - PAD lifecycle
> - audience → relationship/opportunity
> - prospect → client
> - client delivery/value
> - portfolio/owned public experience
> - Customer Intelligence
> - proof → future Positioning/Offer
> - correction → changed future behavior
> - learning → procedural memory
> - transactive routing
> - commercial continuation
> 
> Do not force these to survive.
> 
> Add other journeys where independent evidence supports them.
> 
> ---
> 
> ## I-12 Revenue priority
> 
> Venkat's current near-term revenue priority may affect sequencing.
> 
> Do not allow near-term revenue to redefine the enduring OS purpose without evidence.
> 
> Test whether previous audit work treated revenue only as a constraint rather than as a value path requiring active capabilities.
>

> # PASS J — REPOSITORY RESEARCH / REFERENCE TRANSFER
>

> 
> For every material reference pattern record:

> - valuable outcome
> - relevance to Venkat
> - evidence strength
> - where analogy breaks
> - whether it supports:

>   - rejection
>   - no material implication

> - Wyndo
> - AI Maker
> - Build in Public references
> - other creator/public-work/business/product/experience research
>

> Do not promote reference material into canon.

> 
> # PASS K — TARGETED EXTERNAL RESEARCH IF REQUIRED
> 
> Only if a material question cannot be adequately understood from:
> 
> 1. canon
> 2. repository evidence
> 3. repository research
> 4. qualified current-state evidence
> 
> perform bounded external research if available.
> 
> Prefer primary sources.
> 
> Research the mechanism, not popularity.
> 
> Record:
> 
> - question
> - source
> - mechanism
> - relevance
> - limitations
> - analogy break
> - evidence status
> 
> Do not let external research become repository canon.
> 
> If external research access is unavailable, retain Unknown.

> # PASS L — GENERATION GAP TEST
> 
> Compare the original frozen independent candidate set with candidates exposed by:
> 
> - full repo census
> - audit history
> - conversation history
> - advisor challenge
> - Provided hypotheses
> - research
> 
> For every apparent missing material candidate ask:
> 
> 1. Was it absent from independent generation?
> 2. Was supporting evidence legitimately available?
> 3. Was the evidence excluded by source-aperture design?
> 4. Was it introduced only by a Provided hypothesis?
> 5. Does independent evidence support the underlying need?
> 
> If a material candidate was missed despite legitimate evidence:
> 
> label:
> 
> GENERATION GAP
> 
> Do NOT merely append the candidate.
> 
> Run a fresh bounded independent derivation using the legitimate expanded evidence set while withholding:
> 
> - advisor conclusion
> - Provided recommendation
> - desired target architecture
> 
> Compare the resulting independent candidate with the challenge candidate.
> 
> Document whether it survives.
> 
> This is mandatory.
>

> # PASS M — CROSS-REFERENCE COMPLETENESS
> 
> Perform reciprocal checks.
> 
> ## Full repository → audit
> 
> Every material repository cluster must end with one disposition:
> 
> - represented in Phase 2 current state
> - represented in Phase 2.5 outcome/application
> - supporting evidence
> - research/reference only
> - historical/superseded
> - intentionally deferred
> - rejected
> - no material implication
> - Unknown / requires investigation

> No material cluster may silently disappear.

>

> - represented implication
> - no material implication
> - rejected implication
> - Unknown
> - explicit exclusion reason

> 
> OR

>

> - ownership treatment
> - authority treatment

> ---

> ---

>

> - deliberately excluded

> - contradicted by higher authority
> - unresolved
> - generation-gap candidate

>

> - preventive/detective control
> - whether that control belongs in future methodology

> Do not implement durable controls here.
>

> 
> # QUALIFICATION GATES
> 
> Phase 2.5 cannot be marked QUALIFIED unless all applicable checks pass.
> 
> ## Full repository coverage
> 
> 1. Entire repository was inventoried before relevance filtering.
> 2. Every meaningful top-level area has a disposition.
> 3. Every material artifact cluster has a disposition.
> 4. Unexpected clusters were investigated.
> 5. Cross-directory relationships were tested.
> 6. Highly connected artifacts were inspected.
> 7. Bridge artifacts were inspected.
> 8. Current implementation was checked where it materially confirms or contradicts audit claims.
> 9. Historical/superseded material was separated from current authority.
> 10. Useful historical reasoning was not silently discarded.
> 11. No material source was excluded merely because it was outside the expected audit path.
> 12. Every material cluster has a final disposition.
> 
> ## Source coverage
> 
> 13. Every material source family has a disposition.
> 14. Current and historical evidence are separated.
> 15. Reference research is not confused with canon.
> 16. No material source silently vanished.
> 
> ## Independent derivation
> 
> 17. Independent candidate generation occurred before conversation/advisor/Provided challenge.
> 18. Independent relationship analysis occurred before later challenge material.
> 19. Independent journey discovery occurred before Provided journey hypotheses.
> 20. Frozen outputs and hashes exist.
> 21. Independent disconfirmation occurred.
> 
> ## Relationship depth
> 
> 22. Every material Phase 2.5 candidate received at least a 2-hop upstream search.
> 23. Every material Phase 2.5 candidate received at least a 2-hop downstream search.
> 24. Every material Phase 2.5 candidate received a cross-stream search.
> 25. Third-hop analysis occurred where consequence required it.
> 26. Unsupported relationships were not manufactured.
> 
> ## Journeys
> 
> 27. Material journeys were independently derived.
> 28. Every material journey maps to qualified value or has an explicit disposition.
> 29. Every material transition has relationship/state/ownership/authority treatment.
> 30. Feedback and return loops were tested.
> 31. Journeys were not assumed to be linear.
> 
> ## Cross-reconciliation
> 
> 32. Repository → audit reconciliation passes.
> 33. Source → Outcome reconciliation passes.
> 34. Outcome → Relationship reconciliation passes.
> 35. Relationship → Journey reconciliation passes.
> 36. Journey → Relationship reconciliation passes.
> 37. Outcome → Journey reconciliation passes.
> 38. Journey → Outcome reconciliation passes.
> 39. No material orphan remains unexplained.
> 40. No material sink remains unexplained.
> 41. Cross-stream effects are represented or explicitly absent.
> 
> ## Audit learning
> 
> 42. Prior reruns were examined.

> # TASK B — Independent outcome/application discovery
> 
> ## B1. Valuable CURRENT outcomes to preserve
> 
> 1. **Restartable work.** Four days of operation reconstructible entirely from the file trail; sessions resume from NOW/cards/receipts. Observed (P2v2-C01 qualified; my own reconstruction of Aug 11–14 from receipts confirms it independently).
> 2. **Real-life consequential wins routed through the system:** the taxes GO decision inside the promised day, and the ~$580K condo item surfaced by question protocol — "the largest money item anywhere in the record" (loop #8); the deposit premise corrected, ending dread accrued against a stale mental model (PROFILE corrections log).
> 3. **The publishing unblock.** Zero published artifacts in ten years → one, live, with the PDF unchanged, produced by talk-to-draft with his final voice pass (case-log 2026-08-13; post file). Preserve the exact mechanism: never a blank page; he rewrites; 90-minute cap.
> 4. **Evidence discipline that catches its own builder.** Commit overclaim caught by fresh-context check (Day-1); hook gaps found within the hour of the build; blinding layers each catching a distinct leak class; era-vocabulary false-zero corrected and turned into a rule. Observed across receipts.
> 5. **The blinded evidence layer.** Client-grade proof (platform decks, SOWs, the rare-disease report) made shareable without names, with honest identifiability residuals recorded (`reference/blinded-summaries/`).
> 6. **Positioning raw material with counts.** POV claims each carry quote + source + n; the through-line ("he tests capability, not claims") matches, from the other direction, what his buyers ask ("someone tell me what actually exists") — `state/transcripts/2026-08-13-revenue/INDEX.md`.
> 7. **Honest revenue targeting.** Situation classes labeled by evidence strength; a three-cell experiment that preserves what each cell would teach; an exclusion list applied before any pool; "nothing" as a legitimate outcome (SITUATIONS.md; RECORD.md; plan v3).
> 8. **Decision protection with recorded ratification** and the "considered and struck" register that stops re-proposals (PLAN.md).
> 9. **Correction capture that changes rules the same day** — the learning loop's front half works (PROFILE corrections log; hard rules 10, 13–17 each born from dated failures).
> 10. **The seed corpus with provenance/attribution classes** — 526 files that would otherwise not exist (rescued from session temp, loop #42 closed).
> 
> ## B2. Negative outcomes that should disappear (evidenced)
> 
> 1. **Concurrent-writer loss:** one receipt overwritten and unrecoverable; shared state files still unprotected (loop #19; P2v2-A02).
> 2. **Stale canonical facts in the two most-read files**, with the committed record asserting the wrong side of one (P2v2-F01).
> 3. **Settled matters re-derived at cost:** five already-decided proposals in one session; the creator research re-answering a closed question at ~3x cost the next day (case-log 2026-08-11; PUBLISHING-SYSTEM skeptic).
> 4. **Corrections that don't propagate:** a parallel session loaded files he had banned; loop #37 and #45 rules absent from the specs that should carry them (loop #41; P2v2-C07).
> 5. **Evidence custody outside the repo:** canonical document chains resting on a third-party app's UUIDs; raw session records on a ~1-year expiry fuse; a transcript garble already in a cited note (queue #10/#12; P2v2-E03/E04).
> 6. **Research agents that orchestrate instead of researching:** four coordinator stalls in one day, one burning 300k+ tokens for nearly nothing (loop #45).
> 7. **Over-merge/compression:** three connection claims killed by him in one afternoon — "the system failed when it compressed before it had established the relationships" (Provided, Aug 14; cultivation pass).
> 8. **Governance overhead inversion:** a 4,000-word plan for a twenty-minute check (his own note, loop #59); 14 absolutes added in one day against 4 conditionals after he asked for less rigidity (case-log 2026-08-13).
> 9. **Unverified status leakage:** a receipt claiming three decisions DECISIONS.md does not contain, uncorrected (loop #40; P2v2-F04).
> 10. **The frozen scheduler:** the weekly review — the only mechanism allowed to reorder anything — has never run, so queue positions and "weekly review" triggers are structurally stuck (P2v2-C08).
> 11. **AI-sounding drafts:** post-1's system draft scanned 100% AI; the 8-hour post traced to five unbounded decisions (loop #37; case-log).
> 12. **System-building displacing work:** his own parking of queue #14 ("added during a session that produced a lot of system-building and no work") names the pattern; loop #55 asks research to arrive as brief prep, not as something he watches.
> 
> ## B3. Candidate new outcomes and applications — the honest search
> 
> Method: purpose (determine → complete → preserve+learn → control, in service of near-term money and durable proof) × existing capabilities (A3) × evidence of demand (his recorded asks, loops, and failures). Findings by category:
> 
> - **AI-native redesign of existing outcomes:** three real candidates (IND-01 diagnostic instrument, IND-02 settled-matter challenge, IND-08 blinding gate as a shared service). Several superficially attractive "redesigns" were rejected as already decided and merely gated (the crew, Telegram, content engine) — those are preservation/sequencing matters, not discoveries.
> - **New applications of existing capabilities:** IND-04 (watch-triggers into the brief), IND-05 (receipt conversion sinks), IND-06 (people memory incl. exclusion custody), IND-09 (vocabulary-bootstrap protocol + giveaway), IND-10 (question-led interview instrument), IND-11 (named-definition cheap test — the skeptic's own recommendation, unexecuted).
> - **Capability recombinations:** IND-07 (raw-record custody archive as the shared substrate under three recorded needs), IND-08 (blinding × publishing × outreach), IND-12 (verdict corpus × eval obligation).
> - **Newly viable outcomes:** IND-03 (verdict-calibrated cultivation — the farmer) and IND-12 (personal-judgment eval harness) — both only viable because the corpus now carries provenance/attribution labels and recorded verdicts at scale.
> - **Outcomes requiring investigation:** IND-13 (cross-session correction propagation — proven need, no evidenced mechanism).
> - **Demonstrated AI-native Innovation currently operating: none found.** My independent read agrees with the qualified Phase 2 conclusion: nothing running today produces a materially new outcome class; the strongest current novelties (layered blinding, verdict-gated compression discipline) are redesigns of discipline, not new outcomes. Two candidates below are honestly labeled Innovation *Hypothesis*, not more.
> 
> Duplicates were merged (cross-store convergence timing was folded into IND-03; the Westin experience gate and the build-story assembly stay in B1 as existing designs; the blinded build-in-public lane and its cheap test are already decided direction under D-110 and the MERGE plan, so they are preservation, not discovery).

> - **Learned/refined distinction:** A calendar-based scaling rule and a signal-based one cannot coexist; the number in the plan is a cap on quality dilution, not a target to hit.

> ## Where conclusions landed (cross-record)

> This document is unusually self-documenting: nearly every major conclusion names its own file destination and the write happens in the same breath, visible as a literal tool call in the transcript.

> # DISCONFIRMATION REPORT — frozen Pass A+B and Pass C+D, Chief of Staff OS completeness audit
> 
> Method: I read the four frozen targets in full, then attacked them against primary evidence — 40+ files opened, ~45 of 126 edges spot-checked (15 of the 19 hop-3 edges), all 12 journeys traced to artifacts, all 13 candidates traced to citations, plus mechanical checks (file counts, md5, git log, CSV parsing). Verdict up front: **the set is unusually honest and survives largely intact — no candidate or journey is killed — but I found one material missing actor/edge, two classification errors, four unflagged contradictions in the primary record that the passes reproduced, a systematic citation defect across ~13 edges, and self-integrity defects in the frozen record itself (claimed 74 steps, actual 79).**
> 
> ---
> 
> ## Q1. Candidates lacking sufficient evidence for claimed status/confidence
> 
> - **IND-12 (low confidence — correctly low, but one citation is a gloss).** The row cites P2v2-F08 for "the corpus of verdicts is unusually machine-usable." F08's actual title is "Uncertainty and provenance documentation is an operating practice, not an aspiration" (`audit/findings-v2.1.csv`) — provenance discipline, not eval-usability. And the verdict corpus is thinner than the row implies: `TASTE.md` holds ~10 entries, **9 of them pointing at one receipt (`receipts/2026-08-11-1848`) from one email thread on one day**, nearly all n=1, in a file whose own rule says "n=1 is a guess, not a rule."
> - **IND-04 (high).** The *need* evidence is solid (P2v2-E06 confirmed; `state/projects/taxes.md` "Watch for: no portal invite by midday Monday, August 17"; loops #28/#9). But "high" rides entirely on the need side; the row's own material unknown concedes the mechanism cannot run without an always-on process. Defensible for an application candidate; flag, not fail.
> - **Everything else checks.** I verified IND-01's counts to the file (`content/briefs/2026-08-13--diagnostic-agent-candidate.md`: 71 turns / 29 meetings / 68 identifiable; his ~2:45am ruling verbatim), IND-02's receipt (`2026-08-11-2344.md` exists and says "The friction arrived tonight, five times"), IND-03's full chain (cultivation pass, farmer entry, PROFILE verdict rule, A-LIVE-031 on disk), IND-05 (loops #15/16/17 verbatim + PUBLISHING-SYSTEM §2), IND-06 (RECORD.md: "Names are in the session records; they do not get listed here"), IND-07 (queue #10/#12 verbatim), IND-08 (receipt 1737's four layers; the x-masked residual in the rare-disease summary), IND-09 (PROFILE 2026-08-12 correction + loop #46 + the six-tools table), IND-10 (both runs), IND-11 (skeptic verdict verbatim), IND-13 (loop #41 verbatim; Phase-1 G7 "Required now / Unresolved").
> 
> ## Q2. Candidates that duplicate one another
> 
> - **No mandatory merges. Two real overlaps understated:**
>   - **IND-03 + IND-12 share one scarce substrate** — his recorded verdicts. IND-03 calibrates merge judgment on them; IND-12 builds an eval on them. Both bets fail the same way (verdict volume never suffices — each row's own counterargument), and the register never notes they draw down the same tiny corpus (~10 TASTE entries + 3 cultivation kills + 5 interview read-verdicts + old seed grades). They should at minimum cross-reference as one verdict-corpus program with two consumers.
>   - **IND-09 partially nests inside IND-13** — IND-09's own counterargument says "the rule as prose plus IND-13-style propagation might be the whole fix." The register states this; it just doesn't draw the structural conclusion.
>   - IND-01 and IND-10 share one mechanism (push-back interviewing packaged as an instrument), pointed at different subjects. Separation is defensible; the shared DNA goes unnamed.
> - **The deeper duplication is with the repo's own queue** (see Q6).
>

> 
> **Verified sound in substance (sample):** REL-011 (D-111 + loop #27 + NOW "fully archived, ready"), REL-014 ("Proposed, not promoted" sections exist verbatim in receipts 1805 and 1737), REL-016/122 ("the system sent nothing" verbatim, both receipts), REL-035 (rules 10/13/14/15–17 each cite dated failures in CLAUDE.md text), REL-056 (loop #51 verbatim), REL-063 (D-110's done text + content card "D-110's own definition of done is unmet"), REL-065 (MERGE: "The D-109 redaction gate is where this check lives" verbatim), REL-069 (content card: "two angles exist, neither approved"), REL-072 (plan v3 frontmatter: "APPROVED by Venkat 2026-08-14, ~15:10 ('go and go')"), REL-077/084 (RECORD verbatim), REL-081 (day-30/45/60 verbatim in plan v3), REL-096 (md5-identical garden-state copies, verified by me), REL-101 (receipt 1737 verbatim + commit 0735c86 in git log), REL-104 (14+35+22+127 verbatim in loops #28/#33; encryption key in #36), REL-106 (residual warning verbatim, x-masked), REL-112 (loop #59 verbatim), REL-113/115 (PLAN queue rules + strike register), REL-117 (receipt 0104: "`/clear` then `/daily-brief`"), REL-119/121 (card + loop #8), REL-124/125 (PROFILE five-cause table; NOW's two stale facts confirmed against loop #53 and git log), REL-029 (38 commits, counted by me).
> - **Defect 1 — systematic off-by-one in every dependency-map citation.** `audit/dependency-map-v2.1.csv` has no row-number column: 26 data rows under 1 header. The register cites file-line numbers as row numbers throughout: REL-006 ("row 9"→data row 8), REL-007 (10→9), REL-020 (4→3), REL-021 (5→4), REL-023 (7→6), REL-025 (8→7), REL-026 (3→2), REL-028 (13→12), REL-045 (16–17→15–16), REL-062 (23→22), REL-102 (22→21), REL-121 (24→23), and **REL-031 cites "row 27" — a row that does not exist** (26 is the last; and row 26 is the case-log→receipt-pointer chain, not the share gate, though PLAN.md's share-gate text independently supports the edge). A live primary-record contradiction the register silently sided on.

('go and go')" and NOW says approved. The INDEX — the traceability mechanism the journey certifies — is itself stale for the most load-bearing plan in the set. Steelman: the excluded ones are *decided* (D-101/D-102/D-108) while the included ones are *earned-but-unapproved* (rule 15) — a defensible line, but it is never stated, and "independent discovery" overstates what are requalifications of recorded needs. > ## Q8. Recurrence claims that are repeated copies of one event
> 
> - **"~15 dated corrections-log entries over 4 days" (A4; REL-033) — inflated.** `context/PROFILE.md`'s corrections log holds **11** entries. You reach ~15 only by co-counting dated rules in the short list — a different section of a different kind. Recurrence is still real at n=11; the number is wrong.
> - **"Fresh-context disproof of done: ≥3 dated runs" (A4) — inflated.** Two verifiable events: the Day-1 subagent (receipt 1805, the only receipt containing one — I grepped all 16) and the hook check (loop #58/PLAN, same hour as the build). The CD cycle list honestly names only those two. "≥3" has no third citation anywhere in the allowed corpus.
> - **"6 plans approved &amp; run Aug 13–16" — five verifiable** in `plans/INDEX.md` (automations v2, revenue v3, seed-capture, cultivation, phase-2.5); slide-scan-pilot is Draft; v1/v2s are supersessions of the same efforts.
> - **"≥4 diamond runs" — survives, and is under-claimed:** PUBLISHING-SYSTEM skeptic (Aug 12), MERGE rounds one and two (genuinely distinct lane-sets, ~3am and ~4am Aug 13 — I read both), SITUATIONS-lanes (Aug 14), and the daily review's "Four research diamonds ran overnight."

>

The outcome content (re-derivation cost stops; a compounding translation library) lives in their value_mechanism fields — mislabeled column, not missing substance.
> - **B1-8 and B1-9 are practices, not outcomes:** "decision protection with recorded ratification" and "correction capture that changes rules same day" name mechanisms; their outcomes (settled stays settled; fewer repeated corrections) are unmeasured — and A10-8 itself concedes DONE §10's metrics have no baseline anywhere. The preserve list survives because the practices demonstrably ran, but two of ten B1 entries would fail the pass's own outcome-vs-mechanism test as written.
> - IND-04/06/07 are labeled "Application" — correct handling of mechanism-shaped items.
> 
> ## Q10. Applications that are architecture disguised as capability
> 
> - **IND-07 is the one real instance.** A raw-record custody archive is storage architecture; its stated AI role ("filters/redacts, indexes") is a thin wrapper on a copy job. Its legitimacy rests on two recorded queue needs, and its own counterargument carries D-106's anti-premature-storage warning — but as an "application of existing capabilities" it is the weakest fit in the register.

> ## Q11. AI-native classifications — the direct challenge
> 
> - **IND-03 (Innovation Hypothesis): survives as *hypothesis*, but the innovation content is fully unevidenced.** What exists today: one manual pass, three kills, one refinement, n≈3 verdicts. Everything up to "his verdicts are recorded" is a Redesign of cultivation discipline; the *only* innovation claim is the delegated-judgment step ("repeated verdicts teach which edge classes can move without him") — zero current evidence, and equally describable as ordinary feedback-loop Redesign. > - **IND-12 (Innovation Hypothesis): the label is wrong.** The claimed new outcome — judgment testable against his taste — is **already specified inside the system**: TASTE.md's validation section (blind A/B monthly, rejection replay, edit-trend) and D-103/D-109's *required* two-model eval at Operational v1. Executing an existing spec and a decided obligation is Redesign at most, arguably just implementation. The genuinely novel sliver (a personal-verdict eval set as a reusable artifact) sits on ~10 mostly-n=1 entries from one day. The row's low confidence and counterargument already concede the honest version is "run the overdue blind A/B once." Reclassify.

> - revenue
> - proof
> - experience
> - governance
> 
> 7. Judge frontier innovation using a two-stage evidence standard.
> 
> A genuinely new AI-native Innovation Hypothesis does not require prior case studies or prior evidence that the exact outcome already existed as a recognized need.
> 
> The evidence standard for starting differs from the evidence standard for continuing, expanding, automating, or institutionalizing.
> 
> ---
> 
> # CORE AUDIT CORRECTION
> 
> Treat this as the governing methodological correction for this supplemental run:
> 
> Phase 2.5 contains two different discovery modes.
> 
> ## Mode 1: Evidence-derived outcome discovery
> 
> Question:
> 
> What valuable outcomes are supported by current and historical evidence?
> 
> This work already exists in the frozen audit lineage.
> 
> Do not rerun it.
> 
> ## Mode 2: Frontier AI-native innovation discovery
> 
> Question:
> 
> What valuable outcomes or applications become newly possible because AI participates in reasoning, judgment, exploration, synthesis, memory, tool use, iteration, and learning from the beginning?
> 
> Historical evidence does not define or bound the initial search space.
> 
> Historical and current evidence instead provide:
> 
> - facts
> - capabilities
> - constraints
> - known failures
> - authority boundaries
> - assets
> - prior judgments
> - safety limits
> - provenance
> - material contradictions
> - ingredients available for recombination
> 
> Evidence enters again during qualification.
> 
> Absence from the evidence-derived pass is provenance information.
> 
> It is not negative evidence about the potential value of a frontier innovation.
> 
> Do not write statements such as:
> 
> "Two independent passes did not derive this, therefore the innovation is weaker."
> 
> The valid conclusion is:
> 
> "The evidence-derived passes did not originate this candidate."
> 
> Nothing more follows unless separate evidence supports it.
> 
> ---
> 
> # AI-NATIVE INNOVATION DEFINITION
> 
> For this run, distinguish carefully:
> 
> ## Automation
> 
> AI or deterministic software executes an established outcome with less manual work.
> 
> The outcome remains substantially unchanged.
> 
> ## AI-native Redesign
> 
> AI materially changes how an established valuable outcome is produced.
> 
> The result is still recognizably the inherited outcome.
> 
> ## AI-native Innovation
> 
> AI participation makes a materially new valuable outcome or application possible.
> 
> The novelty must live in the application or outcome, not merely in speed, cost, convenience, quality, or scale.
> 
> ## AI-native Innovation Hypothesis
> 
> A plausible materially new outcome or application whose value has not yet been demonstrated.
> 
> Lack of historical precedent does not count against it merely because the application is new.
> 
> ## None found
> 
> A valid result.
> 
> Do not manufacture innovation.
> 
> ---
> 
> # TWO-STAGE EVIDENCE STANDARD
> 
> ## Evidence required to START a bounded first implementation
> 
> A frontier AI-native Innovation Hypothesis should satisfy all of these:
> 
> 1. Meaningful potential outcome.
> 2. Plausible AI-enabled mechanism connecting capability to outcome.
> 3. Material novelty in the application or outcome.
> 4. Fit with the Chief of Staff OS purpose.
> 5. No material contradiction with known evidence or governing constraints.
> 6. Bounded downside.
> 7. Proportionate implementation cost.
> 8. Human authority remains appropriate for consequential decisions.
> 9. The smallest real implementation still expresses the innovation.
> 10. The first one or two uses produce useful learning.
> 
> Prior case studies of the exact innovation are not required.
> 
> Existing proof of the exact need is not required when the outcome itself did not previously exist.
> 
> ## Evidence required to CONTINUE
> 
> Require early observed signals that the outcome:
> - produces useful value
> - teaches something important
> - reveals unexpected promise
> - or merits another bounded iteration
> 
> ## Evidence required to EXPAND
> 
> Require repeated real use showing sufficient:
> - value
> - reliability
> - demand or usefulness
> - learning
> - manageable burden
> - safety
> 
> ## Evidence required to INSTITUTIONALIZE
> 
> Require stronger evidence concerning:
> - reliability
> - ownership
> - governance
> - maintenance
> - portability
> - failure recovery
> - cost
> - authority
> - long-term usefulness
> 
> Do not collapse these four thresholds into one.
> 
> ---
> 
> # WESTIN COOKIE EXPERIENCE
> 
> Treat Westin as a governing experience standard.
> 
> Do not ask:
> 
> "Has the market validated Westin?"
> 
> The relevant question is:
> 
> "Did this interaction make the recipient feel considered and leave them with something genuinely useful?"
> 
> External experience supplies evidence about execution and adaptation.
> 
> It does not determine whether the Westin standard deserves to exist.
> 
> When frontier candidates concern external experience, ask how AI participation might make this level of consideration possible more consistently without making the recipient feel automated, surveilled, overpersonalized, or burdened.
> 
> ---
> 
> # IMPORTANT CONTROL: GENERATION AND EVALUATION STAY SEPARATE
> 
> Do not let the skeptic suppress frontier generation before candidates have formed.
> 
> Generation is allowed to make aggressive, unusual, non-obvious leaps.
> 
> Evaluation happens afterward in a separate fresh context.
> 
> Generation asks:
> 
> "What becomes possible?"
> 
> Evaluation asks:
> 
> "Does this possibility deserve a bounded encounter with reality?"
> 
> ---
> 
> # REASONING LENSES FOR FRONTIER EXPLORATION
> 
> These are lenses, not fixed agent roles.
> 
> The orchestrator should select only the lenses relevant to the object under investigation.
> 
> ## First principles
> 
> Ignore the inherited workflow.
> 
> What valuable result is ultimately sought?
> 
> What would we design if the old process never existed?
> 
> ## Constraint removal
> 
> Which limitation created the old process or old outcome?
> 
> Examples:
> - human attention
> - processing time
> - search cost
> - memory limits
> - coordination cost
> - inability to monitor continuously
> - inability to synthesize enough evidence
> - inability to personalize at reasonable effort
> - inability to explore enough alternatives
> - inability to preserve context
> 
> What becomes possible when AI materially changes that constraint?
> 
> ## Inversion
> 
> What assumption about impossibility, cost, timing, scale, coordination, or human effort are we carrying forward because it used to be true?
> 
> Try to disprove it.
> 
> ## Counterfactual AI-native design
> 
> If an intelligent participant had existed when this work was invented, would anyone have designed the current workflow or outcome?
> 
> What would have been different from day one?
> 
> ## Capability recombination
> 
> What new outcome appears when:
> - capability A
> - evidence B
> - judgment C
> - workflow D
> - memory E
> - or an external capability
> 
> are combined?
> 
> Do not assume current folder or branch boundaries define useful combinations.
> 
> ## Lateral transfer
> 
> Search for mechanisms from unrelated fields that solve a structurally similar problem.
> 
> Transfer the mechanism, not the surface form.
> 
> Record where the analogy breaks.
> 
> ## Adjacent possibility
> 
> Given what now exists, what becomes newly reachable one step away?
> 
> ## Second-order application discovery
> 
> If innovation X exists, what becomes possible because X now exists?
> 
> This is explicitly allowed.
> 
> PAD is allowed to operate on another AI-native Innovation Hypothesis.
> 
> Limit recursive exploration to one additional level in this run so the search remains bounded.
> 
> ## Recipient-value exploration
> 
> What valuable outcome might the recipient receive that they would not know to request?
> 
> This is especially relevant to:
> - Westin
> - public reasoning
> - Publishing
> - proof
> - relationships
> - revenue
> 
> ## Temporal redesign
> 
> What:
> - disappears
> - moves earlier
> - happens continuously
> - becomes prepared before Venkat's attention
> - becomes observable sooner
> - becomes reversible sooner
> - becomes learnable sooner
> 
> when AI participates from the beginning?
> 
> ---
> 
> # RESEARCH-INFORMED PAD V2 PRINCIPLES
> 
> Track B should study and selectively transfer mechanisms from current systems.
> 
> Do not copy their structure blindly.
> 
> Research mechanism first.
> 
> At minimum examine primary or repository sources for:
> 
> ## Anthropic multi-agent Research
> 
> Study:
> - dynamic decomposition
> - LeadResearcher/orchestrator behavior
> - independent specialist exploration
> - parallel search
> - breadth-first then selective depth
> - clear worker objectives and boundaries
> - adaptive follow-up based on intermediate findings
> - synthesis after independent exploration
> - separate evidence/citation handling
> - scaling effort to problem complexity
> - avoidance of rigid agent teams
> 
> Do not claim Anthropic uses first principles, inversion, or lateral thinking unless its source explicitly says so.
> 
> Those reasoning lenses are part of this experimental PAD design, not claims about Anthropic.
> 
> ## Anthropic agent and Skill patterns
> 
> Study:
> - agents for open-ended tasks
> - environment feedback
> - iterative Skill creation/evaluation
> - separation of stable procedures from model judgment
> - dynamic context loading
> 
> ## Content and marketing systems
> 
> Review representative current systems, including relevant GitHub implementations.
> 
> Look for mechanisms such as:
> - strategy separated from execution
> - research before creation
> - parallel specialist analysis
> - separate critic or QA ownership
> - persistent learning from previous runs
> - observe → act → review loops
> - cross-campaign or cross-artifact intelligence
> - learning redistribution
> - content as an input to later work rather than a dead-end output
> 
> Possible systems to verify include:
> - Claude Blog
> - AI Marketing Claude
> - Digital Marketing Pro
> - Growth Lab
> - Microsoft Agent Framework marketing examples
> - other stronger current examples found during the research
> 
> Record:
> - source
> - current repository state
> - mechanism
> - claimed outcome
> - evidence strength
> - what is transferable
> - where the analogy breaks
> 
> GitHub stars, followers, forks, popularity, or creator audience size are context only.
> 
> Do not use popularity as proof that a mechanism is correct.
> 
> Use primary sources where available.
> 
> External research in this supplemental run is explicitly approved by Venkat.
> 
> ---
> 
> # REQUIRED EXPERIMENTAL DESIGN
> 
> There are TWO PAD tracks plus ONE blinded frontier-control lane.
> 
> The control lane is not a third PAD design.
> 
> It exists to test whether either PAD approach contributes something beyond a general frontier-innovation search.
> 
> All generation lanes must use fresh contexts.
> 
> Do not let one lane see another lane's outputs before freeze.
> 
> ---
> 
> # LANE 0: BLINDED FRONTIER CONTROL
> 
> Purpose:
> 
> Generate frontier AI-native innovation candidates without seeing PAD, PAD v2, Westin as an innovation hypothesis, Advisor innovation proposals, or prior frontier conclusions.
> 
> Allowed inputs:
> - qualified OS purpose
> - relevant canon
> - qualified Phase 1
> - qualified Phase 2
> - current operational evidence
> - existing capabilities
> - important constraints
> - relevant evidence from Bundles 1 through 6 that does not reveal PAD hypotheses
> - current AI capability facts from the verified research packet, stripped of PAD conclusions
> 
> Forbidden:
> - current PAD definition
> - PAD hypotheses
> - PAD v2 design
> - prior PAD conclusions
> - Advisor innovation hypotheses
> - this prompt's PAD-specific expected outcomes
> - outputs from Lane A or B
> 
> Give the worker the frontier reasoning lenses above without telling it about PAD.
> 
> Ask it to search across every approved OS priority area.
> 
> Freeze output before any other lane enters.
> 
> ---
> 
> # LANE A: CURRENT PAD BASELINE
> 
> Purpose:
> 
> Run current PAD exactly as it exists today.
> 
> Do not improve it.
> 
> Do not insert the new research findings.
> 
> Do not rewrite its reasoning method.
> 
> First identify the current PAD source artifacts and their version/status.
> 
> Use explicit current/supersession evidence.
> 
> If current PAD authority is ambiguous, record the ambiguity.
> 
> Do not silently choose among competing PAD definitions.
> 
> Run current PAD against the same substantive starting packet used for Track B.
> 
> Freeze output.
> 
> This is the baseline.
> 
> ---
> 
> # LANE B: RESEARCH-INFORMED PAD V2
> 
> Purpose:
> 
> Test an experimental PAD design optimized for frontier application discovery.
> 
> Use the same substantive starting packet as Lane A.
> 
> Add:
> - verified research-transfer mechanisms
> - frontier reasoning lenses
> - dynamic orchestration
> 
> ## Dynamic orchestration model
> 
> A lead explorer first studies the opportunity object.
> 
> It decides which specialist explorations are worth running.
> 
> It should not launch every lens by default.
> 
> Each worker receives:
> - one clear exploration objective
> - bounded source context
> - relevant tools or references
> - expected output shape
> - what not to investigate
> - what uncertainty to preserve
> 
> Run useful explorations independently and in parallel where practical.
> 
> Possible exploration jobs include:
> - remove inherited constraints
> - first-principles outcome reconstruction
> - cross-capability recombination
> - lateral mechanism transfer
> - recipient-value discovery
> - adjacent possibilities
> - inversion
> - counterfactual AI-native redesign
> - second-order applications
> 
> These are examples.
> 
> The lead chooses what the specific object needs.
> 
> After workers return:
> 
> 1. Preserve their raw candidate outputs.
> 2. Synthesize without erasing provenance.
> 3. Deduplicate only genuine duplicates.
> 4. Preserve disagreements.
> 5. Identify gaps.
> 6. Decide whether one additional bounded exploration round is warranted.
> 7. Stop when another round is unlikely to change a material candidate.
> 
> Do not equate more agents with better exploration.
> 
> Complexity must earn its place.
> 
> Freeze output.
> 
> ---
> 
> # COMMON INPUT OBJECTS
> 
> The frontier search must span the approved priority areas:
> 
> 1. public reasoning
> 2. Publishing
> 3. PAD itself
> 4. research and intelligence
> 5. memory and learning
> 6. executive coordination
> 7. relationships
> 8. revenue
> 9. proof
> 10. experience
> 11. governance
> 
> Search each area independently enough to avoid one domain dominating the others.
> 
> Then search across areas for recombinations.
> 
> Examples of cross-area exploration:
> 
> - memory × public reasoning
> - research × revenue
> - proof × relationships
> - learning × governance
> - executive coordination × research
> - PAD × another Innovation Hypothesis
> - experience × proof
> - Publishing × relationship learning
> - governance × continuous reasoning
> - memory × recipient preparation
> 
> These examples do not imply preferred answers.
> 
> ---
> 
> # CONTENT-SPECIFIC PAD QUESTION
> 
> Do not assume the end product of content work is content.
> 
> For any material:
> - reasoning
> - research
> - draft
> - observation
> - case
> - failure
> - conversation
> - proof artifact
> - seed
> - public response
> 
> ask:
> 
> "What useful application is present here beyond expression?"
> 
> Possible output classes are unconstrained.
> 
> A candidate might become:
> - a decision instrument
> - diagnostic
> - service behavior
> - workflow
> - data product
> - interactive artifact
> - research instrument
> - evaluation mechanism
> - agent capability
> - relationship aid
> - market signal
> - learning mechanism
> - something not represented in this list
> 
> Do not force any output class.
> 
> ---
> 
> # SECOND-ORDER PAD TEST
> 
> After initial qualification, take every surviving AI-native Innovation or Innovation Hypothesis that clears the bounded-start gate.
> 
> Run one additional research-informed PAD v2 pass on that innovation itself.
> 
> Question:
> 
> "Because this innovation now exists, what newly valuable application becomes possible?"
> 
> Stop after one recursive level.
> 
> Record whether second-order PAD:
> - finds nothing
> - finds a derivative application
> - finds a more valuable application than the parent
> - reveals the parent as infrastructure rather than the main outcome
> - reveals a recombination with another OS capability
> 
> This step directly tests whether PAD adds value when applied to existing AI-native Innovation Hypotheses.
> 
> ---
> 
> # BLIND EVALUATION
> 
> After Lane 0, Lane A, and Lane B are frozen:
> 
> Create a fresh evaluator context.
> 
> Hide track identity.
> 
> Randomize candidate order.
> 
> Normalize only formatting.
> 
> Do not rewrite substance.
> 
> The evaluator judges each candidate against the same criteria.
> 
> ## Evaluation questions
> 
> 1. Is the outcome materially different from an inherited outcome?
> 2. Is AI participation essential or materially enabling?
> 3. Is the value meaningful if the candidate works?
> 4. Is the application genuinely new, or old work with AI attached?
> 5. Does it fit OS purpose?
> 6. Does known evidence materially contradict it?
> 7. Is the first real implementation bounded?
> 8. Is implementation cost proportionate to the learning?
> 9. Will the first one or two uses produce useful evidence?
> 10. Is consequential authority preserved?
> 11. What is the strongest counterargument?
> 12. What result would reverse the judgment?
> 
> Do not score candidates merely because they sound novel.
> 
> Do not favor candidates that resemble known software products.
> 
> Do not penalize candidates because no prior case study exists.
> 
> ---
> 
> # CLASSIFICATION
> 
> Each material candidate receives TWO separate judgments.
> 
> ## Judgment 1: AI-native classification
> 
> One of:
> 
> - Automation
> - AI-native Redesign
> - AI-native Innovation
> - AI-native Innovation Hypothesis
> - None found
> - Unclassified / insufficient basis
> 
> ## Judgment 2: implementation eligibility
> 
> One of:
> 
> - Do not start
> - Clears bounded first-implementation gate
> - Continue based on early evidence
> - Expand
> - Institutionalize
> 
> Most new candidates should not begin above the first two statuses.
> 
> Do not confuse implementation eligibility with proof of value.
> 
> ---
> 
> # REQUIRED CANDIDATE FIELDS
> 
> For every material frontier candidate capture:
> 
> candidate_id
> lane_origin
> name
> priority_area
> cross_area_recombination
> beneficiary
> new_outcome_or_application
> inherited_outcome_if_any
> pre_ai_constraint
> what_changed_with_ai
> ai_role
> why_this_was_impractical_or_unavailable_before
> capabilities_recombined
> frontier_reasoning_path
> value_mechanism
> known_evidence_used_as_constraint
> known_contradictions
> ai_native_classification
> current_evidence_status
> implementation_eligibility
> smallest_real_implementation
> first_use_case
> second_use_case
> learning_produced
> stop_condition
> continue_condition
> expand_condition
> institutionalize_condition
> human_authority_boundary
> downside
> implementation_cost
> portability_or_lock_in_risk
> strongest_counterargument
> reversal_evidence
> source_provenance
> confidence
> notes
> 
> Do not require an "existing demonstrated need" field for Innovation.
> 
> If a known problem inspired the innovation, record it.
> 
> If the innovation arose from recombination, intuition, adjacency, or frontier exploration rather than a pre-existing articulated need, record that honestly.
> 
> ---
> 
> # TRACK COMPARISON
> 
> After blind evaluation, reveal lane provenance.
> 
> Compare:
> 
> ## Lane 0 vs Lane A
> 
> What did current PAD add beyond general frontier exploration?
> 
> What did it miss?
> 
> ## Lane 0 vs Lane B
> 
> What did research-informed PAD v2 add beyond general frontier exploration?
> 
> What did it miss?
> 
> ## Lane A vs Lane B
> 
> Did research-informed PAD v2 produce:
> - more distinct applications
> - deeper applications
> - more second-order applications
> - less duplication
> - fewer old-outcome disguises
> - better bounded first implementations
> - different classes of value
> 
> Do not declare a winner from candidate counts alone.
> 
> ## Against the frozen evidence-derived Phase 2.5 set
> 
> Identify:
> - convergence
> - genuinely frontier-only candidates
> - evidence-derived candidates that frontier reasoning transforms
> - frontier ideas already present under another name
> - contradictions
> - areas where neither method found anything
> 
> Absence from one track is provenance.
> 
> Do not automatically interpret absence as negative evidence.
> 
> ---
> 
> # ADVERSARIAL CHALLENGE
> 
> Use a separate fresh-context skeptic after comparison.
> 
> Its job is to kill weak frontier ideas.
> 
> For each surviving Innovation or Innovation Hypothesis, attack:
> 
> - novelty theater
> - old outcome disguised as innovation
> - AI not actually necessary
> - unclear beneficiary
> - weak value
> - hidden operational burden
> - privacy or confidentiality problems
> - authority creep
> - maintenance burden
> - provider lock-in
> - brittle dependency
> - inability to learn from early implementation
> - implementation too large for the uncertainty
> - duplication of an existing capability
> - speculative architecture embedded in the outcome
> - mechanism confused with requirement
> - popularity confused with proof
> - current trend confused with durable value
> 
> The skeptic does not require historical proof of the exact innovation.
> 
> Its job is to test coherence, value, boundedness, and reversibility.
> 
> ---
> 
> # REQUIRED RESEARCH-TRANSFER OUTPUT
> 
> Create a bounded reference-transfer artifact that records:
> 
> source
> source_type
> mechanism
> claimed_result
> evidence_strength
> relevance
> transferable_principle
> where_analogy_breaks
> whether_used_in_PAD_v2
> notes
> 
> Keep external research separate from OS evidence.
> 
> External examples support mechanism plausibility.
> 
> They do not prove a candidate will create value for Venkat.
> 
> ---
> 
> # DO NOT DO
> 
> Do not rerun Phase 1.
> 
> Do not rerun Phase 2.
> 
> Do not overwrite Bundles 1 through 6.
> 
> Do not rewrite the frozen A/B, C/D, E, H, I, J, or L artifacts.
> 
> Do not modify the existing Phase 2.5 v2 report or candidate CSV yet.
> 
> Do not run Pass M.
> 
> Do not run Pass N.
> 
> Do not run Phase 3, 4, 5, or 6.
> 
> Do not implement any candidate.
> 
> Do not promote PAD v2 to canon.
> 
> Do not replace current PAD.
> 
> Do not turn exploratory agent roles into permanent OS branches.
> 
> Do not preselect a graph, CRM, database, memory store, funnel, agent crew, or other mechanism.
> 
> Do not assume every priority area needs Innovation.
> 
> Do not force novelty.
> 
> Do not use GitHub stars, follower counts, or popularity as evidence of value.
> 
> Do not treat Advisor statements as current-state evidence.
> 
> Do not treat historical absence as evidence against a genuinely novel application.
> 
> Do not allow a candidate's similarity to a successful external system to substitute for qualification.
> 
> Do not let the skeptic see unfinished raw generation.
> 
> ---
>

> 
> # VALIDATION AND TESTING
> 
> Before reporting completion, run these checks.
> 
> ## File integrity
> 
> - Verify every required output exists.
> - Verify no earlier audit artifact was modified.
> - Verify no Phase 3+ file changed.
> - Record git status before and after.
> - Record hashes of every frozen generation artifact.
> - Preserve the original frozen artifact after hashing.
> 
> ## Context isolation
> 
> For Lane 0 verify:
> - no PAD artifact was opened
> - no PAD output was visible
> - no Advisor innovation hypothesis was visible
> - no prior frontier conclusion was visible
> 
> For Lane A verify:
> - research-informed PAD v2 instructions were not visible
> - Lane B outputs were not visible
> - Lane 0 outputs were not visible
> 
> For Lane B verify:
> - Lane A and Lane 0 outputs were not visible during generation
> 
> Record exact input allowlists for all three.
> 
> ## Comparison integrity
> 
> - Use the same substantive source object for Lane A and Lane B.
> - Record any unavoidable difference.
> - Use comparable exploration budgets.
> - Let Lane B allocate its budget dynamically inside the same overall envelope.
> - Do not interpret raw candidate count as performance.
> 
> ## CSV validation
> 
> Check:
> - expected column count
> - no shifted rows
> - no malformed records
> - unique candidate IDs
> - valid classification values
> - valid implementation-eligibility values
> - no missing provenance for material candidates
> 
> ## Classification validation
> 
> Sample every candidate classified as:
> - Innovation
> - Innovation Hypothesis
> 
> Ask a fresh evaluator:
> 
> "Would this still be a materially new outcome if the AI were merely faster and cheaper?"
> 
> If yes only because of speed, cost, convenience, or scale:
> downgrade it.
> 
> Ask:
> 
> "Did the evaluation reject this candidate only because no prior example exists?"
> 
> If yes:
